Boats arriving from pay full inward dues if discharging at Shasi town, local trade dues if discharging near the Wes Station ; but in the S table they are all entered as local traders. In the same way boats from Shasi town to Tangyang pay full ou dues, but are classed among local clearances. Boats arriving from Tangyang for other ports pay full outward dues. They are entered in the Shipping table first as local entries, then as interport clearances. Boats arriving from other ports for Tangyang have usually no dues to pay, but in the Shipping table they are entered, firstly, as interport entries and, secondly, as local elearances. 11.-1M PORTS. 0. 1913.] NOTIIoATIONs. 291 No. 549. NINGPO DISTRICT. TONGTING ISLAND FOG SIGNAL. Ano or AUDIBILITY. MARINERs are hereby reminded that the fog signal sounded at Tongting Island Light- station is audible only to the eastward of the island, between the bearings of north through west to south. SHANGHAI, 29th April 1913. No. 550. - SHANGHAI DISTRICT. NORTH SADDLE LIGHT-STATION. SUBMARINE FOG BELL PERMANENTLY DISCONTINUED. NOTICE is hereby given that the submarine fog bell off North Saddle Light-station has been permanently discontinued. SHANGHAI, 5th May 1913.
292 oveTous GAZEwnE. [APRIL-JUNE, N o. 551. NEWCHWANG DISTRICT. LIAO RIVER. ESABLISHMaNT OF TWO SPAR Buoys OF EVRanLASTING POINT. NOTIon is hereby given that two red painted spar buoys surmounted by spherical ges have been placed in 8 feet of water at low water of spring tides, to mark the edge ofthe shoal to the westward of Everlasting Point. From the upper spar buoy, Fish House Beacon bears S. 30 W., distant I mile and cables. From the lower spar buoy, Fish House Beacon bears 8. 2 45 W. distant I mile. Bearings given are magnetic. SHNGoAI, 24tle May 1p13. No. 552. TIETS18 DITRICT. GUL OF PEOILI-TAKU BAR. TExPOnAR LADpIxG MAR RPLACED BY PanxANaNT MARKa. NOTIE is lherely given that the temporary ling marks for the Bar Channel have e epT d by two iron open-work beoni erected one on the mainland and one on the foreshore, abouit 1.8 miles to the southward of North Fort Light. The beacons are surmounted by spherical eages and are painted black. The height of the eastern beaeon is 47 feet, and that of the western beacon 68 feet, above high water, and they are 5,ooo feet apart. At night a fixed white light, dioptric, of the sixth order, is exhibited from the summit ofeach beacon. SHANGHAI, 7th Junle 1913.
1913.] NOTIFICATIONS. 298 No. 558. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. CHANGE IN OHARACTER OF SHAWEISHAN LIGHT. REFERRING to Notice to Mariners, No. 544, notice is hereby given that on or about the I5th July 1913 the character of the Shaweishan ight will be changed. The new illuminating appartus wil be group-fashing of the second order showing two white lightning flashes in q nscession every 15 seconds. The approximate power of the flash will be.40o,ooo candles. In all other respects the particulars of this light-station will remain as before. The temporary lights, notified in Notice to Mariners NO. 544, will be discontinued whon the new light is exhibited. SHANGHAI, 12th Jee 1913. No. 554. GULF OF LIAOTUNG-ENTRANCE TO LIA(O RIVER. CHANGE IN POSITIONS OF MIDDLE AND INNER, OR FAIRWAY, Buoys. NoncE is hereby given that the Middle Buoy has been shifted about j cable S.E. from its former position, and is now moored in 8 feet at low water of spring tides. The Inner, or Fairway, Buoy has been shifted about I cable S.E. by E. from its former position, is painted black, and marks the port side of the channel for vessels bound up river. The buoy is now moored in io feet at low. water of spring tides, and will be known as the Inner Buoy. All bearings given are magnetic. SHANGHAI, 30th June 1913.
